# Fragment issues — section 5b, rows 401 to 425

> Rows 401 to 425 of section 5b of [the register](../FRAGMENT-ISSUES.md), in their own file since 2026-10-09 so that
> they can be read alone. **The section's own rules are on that page, under its heading.** A new
> row goes at the end of the section's last file, whatever its number, and the next run of
> [`tools/split-register.py`](../../tools/split-register.py) moves it to the file its number
> belongs to. [`tools/register-text.py`](../../tools/register-text.py) reads every row back into
> the register for every tool that reads it, so a row here is seen exactly as it was seen there.

| # | Defect | State |
|---|---|---|
| **401** | ***"how many rooms are there"* WAS ANSWERED BY `PLACE_ROOMS`, A CHANGE THAT PLACES ROOMS, ON THE TRAINED BACKEND - THE ROOM TWIN OF [ROW 355](#)'S SPACES, AND MOST OTHER WAYS OF ASKING FOR A ROOM COUNT STILL REACH A CHANGE ON ONE BACKEND OR THE OTHER.** Reported to this session on 2026-10-09 and reproduced on scratch stores from `main` at `aebca7ad`, 480 fragments, through `heron_brain.lookup` - the function `heron_lookup` calls - behind the 2024 wall, on both backends: lexical, and `model` (`model2vec` 0.9.0, `minishlab/potion-base-8M`, in a venv outside the repository, warmed and checked before the first question). **On `model`:** `PLACE_ROOMS` (FRG-GEO-014, DRAFT, MODIFY), then `SELECT_UNENCLOSED_ROOMS`, `REPORT_AREA_VOLUME_COMPUTATIONS`, `CHECK_ROOM_MEP_COMPLETENESS` and `COUNT_BY_SPATIAL_CONTAINER` - and none of the five counts rooms. A question answered by a write is the one routing outcome D-86 does not leave to judgement. **On lexical:** `REPORT_AREA_VOLUME_COMPUTATIONS` (FRG-GEO-056, READ), which reads one document setting and counts nothing. It was `COUNT_BY_SPATIAL_CONTAINER` on `be933bf2` and moved with PR #460 ([row 387](#)), read to read, before PR #450 ([row 376](#)) - which expected to move it - had merged; with #450 merged locally it stays where it is. **Which read answers it, from the cards:** not `COUNT_BY_SPATIAL_CONTAINER` - it counts the elements handed in, broken down by the room that contains each: *how many diffusers in each room*, not how many rooms there are. Not `COUNT_ELEMENTS` or `REPORT_ROOM_SPACE_DATA`: both take a set of elements and refuse when nothing is selected (row 355). Not `REPORT_AREA_VOLUME_COMPUTATIONS`. It is `FILTER_ELEMENTS_BY_CATEGORY` (FRG-ELE-001, PROVEN, READ) with category `Rooms` and levelId none - the read row 355 chose for *how many MEP spaces are in the model*, and the filter half of the `count-elements` skill. Its collector has no placement test, so a room that was never placed is counted with the rest. | **FIXED FOR THIS SENTENCE - measured on the search, not run in Revit. THE KIND IS OPEN, as row 355's is.** The filter declares *how many rooms are there* with a dated comment, and both routing tables - the filter's and `PLACE_ROOMS`' - name it. Utterances and comments only: `impl/` is untouched, so the proof fingerprint (`492d8e1fd3344327`, implementation only) holds and the card stays PROVEN. Measured before and after on scratch stores, both backends, on `main` and on `main` with #450 merged: the sentence now answers by identity, to the filter, on all four. **What else moved, of 65 sentences** - room counts, room jobs on the edges, and 30 counts and lists from other domains: on lexical, *how many spaces are there* and *how many spaces are in this model* leave `REPORT_AREA_VOLUME_COMPUTATIONS` for the filter, read to read, and both are now right; on `model`, nothing. **Of 20 ways of asking for a room count** (the first ten written before the card was touched), 6 reach a change on lexical before and after, and 7 then 6 on `model`. The one other move is on `model`: *how many rooms on each level* goes from `SET_ROOM_LIMITS` to `PLACE_ROOMS`, a write to a write, with `REPORT_ROOM_SPACE_DATA` third both times. The new sentence shares *how many rooms* and puts the filter at words #2, so both writes drop one rank there, from an exact fused tie (words #2 + nearness #3 against #3 + #2) to a hair apart (#3 + #3 against #4 + #2). **Still reaching a change, unchanged by this:** *count the rooms* and *count all the rooms* (`SET_ROOM_LIMITS`, lexical); *how many rooms on level 2* (`SET_ROOM_LIMITS`, both); *how many rooms are on this floor* (`CREATE_FROM_ROOM_BOUNDARIES`, both); *how many rooms are there in the model* (`PLACE_ROOMS`, `model`); *tell me how many rooms this model has* and *are there any rooms in this model* (`PLACE_ROOMS`, both); *how many rooms on each level* (`model`). **Seen while measuring, not this row's to fix:** *list the rooms* and *show me all the rooms* reach `PLACE_ROOMS` on both backends; *which rooms are missing services*, which `COUNT_BY_SPATIAL_CONTAINER`'s routing table sends to `CHECK_ROOM_MEP_COMPLETENESS`, reaches `PLACE_ROOMS` on both; on `model`, *how many walls are there* reaches `SET_WALL_CONSTRAINTS` and *how many views are there* `SET_VIEW_PROPERTIES`. The kind stays open for row 355's reason: `PLACE_ROOMS`' card sits nearest any question about rooms or spaces, and the fix is a re-design of it measured on both backends, or reopening row 109's "warn, don't re-rank" - the owner's call, not a wording tweak. **Gates, before and after, both backends:** `check-routing` - the same exit, no question answered by a write, the claimed-and-not-reached list unchanged (21 lexical, 19 `model`), one new contest - this sentence on ranking alone, the filter #7 - and two rank ripples of one; `check-risk-crossings`, `score-routing` (the owner's questions) and `check-skill-routing` - the same answer question for question; `check-intrusion` - the same exit, `PLACE_ROOMS` in one more shortlist on `model` (67 to 68), and on lexical the twelfth place is `SET_AIR_TERMINAL_FLOW` at 50 shortlists where it was `DIMENSION_ROOMS` at 50. **Repeated on `main` `36f02b54`** (481 fragments, after #455, #459 and #450 merged) against this branch merged into it, both backends: the same answers and the same moves in all 77 probe sentences; the five tools move as they did on `aebca7ad`, except that `check-routing`'s two rank ripples are already on that `main`, so this adds none; on lexical `check-intrusion`'s twelfth place is `SET_AIR_TERMINAL_FLOW` at 49 where `main` has `DIMENSION_ROOMS` at 50; and the eleven CI gates exit 0 on the merged tree. **Repeated again on `main` `a2e00c7a`** (484 fragments, after #443, #445, #446, #449, #465 and #466) against a trial merge of this branch, lexical in a clean venv and `model` in one without the re-ranker the owner's laptop now runs live: the sentence answers by identity to the filter on both; on lexical *how many spaces are there* moves to the filter (that `main` already sends *how many spaces are in this model* there); on `model` *how many rooms on each level* goes from `SET_ROOM_LIMITS` to `PLACE_ROOMS`, as before; of the 20 room counts, 6 reach a change on lexical before and after, and 8 then 7 on `model`; `check-routing`'s ripples there are *find all elements of a category* #7 to #10 and *set the room number* #48 to #47, and the other four tools move as before; the eleven CI gates exit 0 on the trial merge. An identity answer comes before any ranking, so the re-ranker cannot change this sentence's answer; what it does to the others was not measured. **Two new crossings on that `main`, the same with and without this change:** *how high are these rooms* reaches `PLACE_ROOMS` on lexical, where `36f02b54` gave `SELECT_UNENCLOSED_ROOMS`, and *how many rooms have been placed* reaches `CENTER_ROOM_TAGS`, a write, on `model`, where it gave `REPORT_DOOR_ROOM_LINKS`. The live `heron_lookup` reads the main checkout's cards, so its answer changes once this is merged and that checkout is brought level. |
| **402** | **`tests/test_mcp_serves.py` DOES NOT RETURN INSIDE `check-gaps`' 300-SECOND CEILING IN A CLOUD CONTAINER, SO `check-gaps` LISTS IT UNFINISHED.** Found 2026-10-09 running `python tools/check-gaps.py` end to end for the first time in the [project review](../work-notes/plans/project-review-2026-10-08.md) (at low priority, beside two other agents' builds, on 4 CPUs): *HUNG test_mcp_serves.py - still running after 300s, gave up*. Timed side by side under the same load: `main` at 661384f6 took 445 s and this branch 562 s, both exit 0. So the suite was already over the ceiling before this branch's new sections (row 5b-233's lookup, row 5b-274's activity list) were added; they add about a quarter. `SUITE_TIMEOUT` in [`tools/check-gaps.py`](../../tools/check-gaps.py) says 300 s is about four times the slowest suite measured there, 78 s on 2026-09-10. NOT measured on an unloaded machine or on the owner's PC. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** Either the suite is made faster or `check-gaps` gives it a ceiling of its own, and the choice is for whoever owns that rule; raising the one ceiling for every suite would let a hung suite wait longer before it is named. |
| **403** | **THE COMPANION'S PAGE ACTIONS THAT REACH REVIT STILL GUESS HOW A CALL ENDED, OR WRITE REFUSED FOR A REPLY THAT IS NOT OK.** Carried from [row 5b-389](section-5b-rows-376-400.md) on 2026-10-09, when that row's `revit_*` half was fixed. Four page actions in mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py reach Revit and write their own line on the activity list. `_apply_change` (companion_apply) records revit_change's answer with no outcome, so that line is guessed from its first line. It calls `_through(revit_change, origin="companion")` with no reply_out, a line tests/test_companion.py pins as text. `_apply_table` (companion_table_apply) writes `refused` only when the fragment's resultJson says nothing was applied, and no outcome otherwise, so a lost answer or revit_busy is guessed. `_sprinkler_layout_place` (companion_sprinkler_layout_place) writes `refused` for any revit_change reply that is not ok, so a lost placement answer shows as refused. Its good line is guessed, and its earlier refusals write no line. `_load_settings` (companion_load, the page's Load from Revit) writes `refused` for any revit_read reply that is not ok, so a lost answer or revit_busy shows as refused. Its good line is guessed. Its earlier returns (NotBound, list_views not answered, the pin's refusal, no active view) write no line at all. Since row 5b-389, `_through` hands the body's own label back in reply_out["ended"], and that is the hook the repair can use. Read in the source, NOT RUN. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** The repair records the label `_through` hands back in reply_out["ended"] on each page action's line. `_apply_change` needs a reply_out on its `_through` call, which changes the line tests/test_companion.py pins, so the Companion's owner should agree to that first. |
| **404** | **THE LOOKUP'S FIRST OPEN REBUILDS AN EMPTY SHARED STORE FROM WHICHEVER CHECKOUT ASKS, AND `heron_scope.py --rebuild` REBUILDS IT FROM ANY CHECKOUT.** Found 2026-10-09 while closing [row 5b-233](section-5b-rows-226-250.md)'s leftovers. Read in the source, not run. In `mcp/server/heron_brain.py`, `_Open._prepare` calls `heron_scope.rebuild()` whenever the GLOBAL store is empty. `rebuild()` builds from the asking checkout's own cards, and `_prepare` never asks `heron_scope.rebuild_refusal()`. So a new or deleted shared store, first asked from a worktree or from the main folder on a feature branch, is built from that branch's cards. `refresh()` never removes the rows that adds. `python brain/heron_scope.py --rebuild` has no check either. The heron-ship skill tells a person to run it from the main checkout on main, but nothing stops a run from anywhere else. Both are row 5b-233's class: one store every chat on the PC reads, holding whichever checkout last built it. `tools/check-routing.py`'s `store_for_this_tree()`, the tools that call it, and the lookup's warm-up and rebuild advice now refuse it. These two paths do not. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** Someone has to decide the fix: refuse on the shared store, or build it from the main checkout's cards, the ones `refreshes_from()` names. NOT RUN: neither path was run here. |
| **405** | **`COMBINE_FAMILY_FORMS` MEASURED ONLY WHAT REVIT KEPT IN THE COMBINATION - A FORM REVIT LEFT OUT WENT UNSEEN, SO A COIL CAME BACK HALF GONE AS A GOOD CUT, AND A CUT REVIT COULD NOT MAKE CAME BACK AS "NO SOLID IN IT TO MEASURE".** Seen twice on 2026-10-07 in the owner's session (Revit 2024, session 9240): Family1, 24 solid 15-degree revolves of a stacked coil spring plus two end voids, and Family11, a seat extrusion on Center (Front/Back) plus a void on Ref. Level rounding its corners - both threw *"Revit made a combination with no solid in it to measure. NOTHING from this call was kept."* while 2 solids + 4 voids and 1 solid + 5 voids worked in the same session. **Reproduced on a scratch family, never on Family1 or Family11** - `CombineScratch.rfa`, Metric Generic Model, `%TEMP%\heron-work\combine`, every combine rolled back. **The cause, measured:** when Revit's join or cut fails on a form, it leaves that form's material out of the combination and still lists it as a member - after `Regenerate` every member reads 0 L of its own and the combination holds only the rest - and says *"Can't keep elements joined"* only when the transaction commits, after the fragment has measured. A coil of 24 revolves, six circles "circle 60,Z 8" a 24 mm pitch apart, each revolve 1 mm higher than the last, with voids "rect -90,-90 90,90" at Z -20..3.5 and 152.5..176: 26 of 26 listed, ONE solid of 0.2251 L of the 0.4548 L named, every other revolve gone - and the old code reported *"0.455 L before, 0.225 L after - 0.23 L taken away"* and passed; read back after the commit, the combination still held 0.2251 L. A second coil whose revolves overlap by a degree each way had 12 of its 24 left out too. The seat as described cut correctly (27.461 to 27.091 L); with the void's corner arcs typed to whole millimetres ("arc 537,-162 560,-105", its sides still on the seat's faces) Revit left the seat out entirely and main's code threw the owner's exact words; the same void with its sides a millimetre past the faces cut (27.461 to 27.113 L). Not the causes first suspected: no `GeometryInstance` (one Solid, any detail level, non-visible objects included), and two solids apart or touching, and a void that misses, all behave. Also measured: a void's own geometry is an inside-out solid of NEGATIVE volume, Revit's inside/outside answer for one reads backwards, and `BooleanOperationsUtils` cannot stand in as a check - it failed on the coil and ADDED a void's volume to a box. | **FIXED, PROVEN 2026-10-08 - signed by Ajmal PS.** Points inside each solid are taken before combining (one per face, from the chord along its normal); the combination is made first in a sub-transaction, always rolled back, and every point no void covers must lie inside the result - judged by counting face crossings along three skewed rays, which holds for an inside-out void too. A solid left out refuses the call with nothing kept, naming each form and Revit's own words; only then is the real combination made, and read form by form again. "Void took nothing away" and "join cannot add material" unchanged. Measured on `CombineScratch`, rolled back: the coil (both kinds) refused naming the 12 revolves, and a KEPT refusal left 0 combinations; the whole-millimetre seat refused naming the seat; seat 0.371 L, seat 1 mm past 0.348 L, box + hole 0.785 L, box + void that misses still refused, two boxes apart or touching 32 L. Compiles 2020 to 2027. Proof recorded in its card, fingerprint b29898ba6396b9b9: the seat cut 0.371 L and read back; the coil refused naming the 12 revolves, nothing combined. **Also seen, not fixed:** two solids 600 mm apart join with the words "they only touched". **Written as 5b-370, renumbered 405 at the merge:** #444 merged its own 5b-370 first. The fragment's two code comments still say 5b-370 - they are inside the proof seal, so they are corrected at its next re-proof, as 5b-69 set out; the card and its cases say 5b-405. |
| **406** | **A CARD MERGED INTO MAIN NEVER REACHED THE SHARED KNOWLEDGE STORE UNTIL SOMEBODY RAN `python brain/heron_scope.py --rebuild` BY HAND - SO ON THE OWNER'S PC *"list the materials in this family"* COULD NOT REACH `LIST_MATERIALS` AND WENT TO `SET_FAMILY_FORM_MATERIAL`, A CHANGE.** Found 2026-10-10 on the owner's PC: `%APPDATA%\Heron\knowledge\global.db` held 480 fragment rows beside 481 cards in `brain/fragments/`, and the missing one was `list-materials` (FRG-ELE-077, merged in #450 as `36f02b54`). `heron_brain._Open._prepare` calls `heron_scope.rebuild()` only for an EMPTY store and otherwise `refresh()`, which by design never added a row or removed one ([row 249](#)'s *Not done here*; [NEEDS-CHECKING BD3](../needs-checking/group-bd.md)) - and it had already looked at the new card and MARKED it, so every later lookup skipped it without opening it. The owner is a modeller, and a command to remember after every update is a step that does not happen. **Reproduced 2026-10-10 on a scratch shared store** - `APPDATA` pointed at a work folder outside the repository, the real store untouched - built by the code as it stood from main `36f02b54`, FRG-ELE-077's row taken out and its card marked by one refresh, exactly the owner's state. On the trained backend with the cross-encoder re-ranker, *"list the materials in this family"* answered `SET_FAMILY_FORM_MATERIAL` and *"show me all the materials in this project"* `REPORT_PIPE_SEGMENTS`, both by the hybrid route, and the store stayed at 480. | **FIXED 2026-10-10 - in a chat started after this merges.** `refresh()` also asks a new `_library_moves()`. In the SHARED store only, read from a main checkout on branch `main` or from an installed Heron: a card folder no row names is parsed whatever its mark says, and ADDED when it validates; a row whose card folder has GONE from main is DROPPED when that row was made from main's card - its folder is in `rows_from`. Same single short `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, everything slow outside it, and every condition asked again under the lock. **Never:** in a private store - a suite's, CI's, a measurement's; from a worktree ([row 131](#)); from the main folder on a feature branch, whose new cards are unmerged ([row 233](#)'s hazard); a row a session planted for its unmerged card, which was never in `rows_from`; a card whose `fragment.yaml` is missing while its folder is still there, which is git writing it anew; anything at all when main's library holds no card. A NEW card that does not validate is not added, and the lookup carries on; in the shared store it is parsed again on each lookup until it validates, because the file that finishes it may not be `fragment.yaml`. The capability map, the search index and the vectors all read the rows and run after the refresh inside the same `_Open`, so a card added there is found in that same lookup; a dropped row's vector stays in `vectors` - `heron_embed.index` never prunes a fragment's - but every route keeps only the ids the store holds (`heron_retrieve`'s `keep`), as after any rebuild. **The same probe after:** both sentences answer `LIST_MATERIALS` by the **identity** route on the first lookup, the store is at 481, and *"select all ducts"* is unchanged (identity, `FILTER_ELEMENTS_BY_CATEGORY`). A quiet lookup's refresh, 20 runs over the 481 cards: median 3.9 ms after against 4.4 ms before. [`tests/test_scope_store.py`](../../tests/test_scope_store.py) section 5 gains `following_main()` and one private-store check: ten of the new checks fail on the code before, and each guard above, removed alone, turns at least one red (2026-10-10). **Not done here:** a card whose id changed inside its own folder still has a row by folder, so nothing is added and `--rebuild` is still the answer; rows a worktree's `rebuild()` put into the shared store for cards main also holds are not rewritten back ([row 233](#)) - only the cards it added are dropped and the ones it left out added; and a chat started before this merges runs the old code until the Claude app is restarted. #449, merged the same day, added `drift()` - a REPORT of the same folder-name comparison against the asking checkout's cards - and moved `on_main_branch()` into `heron_scope`; this uses that one and keeps `refresh()`'s `walked`, so `drift()` still reads the walk it took. |
| **407** | **`revit_change` HANDS BACK EVERY VARIABLE A WRITE FRAGMENT'S SCRIPT LEFT, NOT THE `provides` ITS CARD DECLARES, AND ENDS EVERY REPLY WITH THE SAME *"was KEPT"* PARAGRAPH - SO ONE AFTERNOON'S TWO FAMILY BUILDS CARRIED 322,725 CHARACTERS, ABOUT 150k TOKENS, BACK INTO THE CHAT, AND ABOUT 70% OF IT SAID NOTHING NEW.** Measured 2026-10-09 building a fire alarm control panel and a duty/standby pump set on the owner's new laptop ([row 5b-378](section-5b-rows-376-400.md) has the session), and re-derived 2026-10-10 from that chat's transcript. **172 `revit_change` replies, 322,725 characters as sent, 314,993 once the JSON is decoded:** 42.0% of the decoded text is lines naming a variable the fragment's card does not declare under `contract.provides` - ``along Func`3``, ``axisOf Func`2``, `invariant CultureInfo`, ``plane Tuple`4``, `inPlane 3 item(s) [System.Int32[], ...]`, `datums 3 item(s)` with each tuple printed under the Revit API's own type name for a reference plane or a level, `halfMillimetre 0.00164041994750656` - and 2.8% more is the fragment's own needs echoed back, about 45% together; 24.5% is the *"'NAME' was KEPT: Revit accepted the transaction rather than rolling it back ..."* paragraph, word for word in 170 of the 172. 127 of the 172 calls were `CREATE_FAMILY_EXTRUSION`, which alone gave 116,590 of the 132,308 undeclared characters; `SET_FAMILY_FORM_MATERIAL`, `SET_FAMILY_TYPE_VALUES`, `COMBINE_FAMILY_FORMS`, `CREATE_FAMILY_REVOLUTION` and `SET_FAMILY_FORM_VISIBILITY` did the same. About 2.2 characters a token, measured on the 12 replies that came back on their own: the context grew 6,968 tokens for their 15,260 characters. **Why, read:** `Report` in [`RevitFragment.cs`](../../revit/Heron.Revit.Addin/RevitFragment.cs) puts every name in `state.Variables` into `provides` except what the host bound and is still the host's (`StillTheHosts`) - the card's declared `provides` is never consulted - and `_provides_lines` in [`heron_mcp_server.py`](../../mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py) prints each one as an aligned line. **Already recorded, and narrower:** [row 304](section-5b-rows-301-325.md) names private helpers returned as results by `REPORT_FAMILY_TEMPLATE` alone, and [a 2026-09-22 note](a-drawing-set-and-a-house-plan-2026-09-22.md) saw ``readValue Func`2`` from `read-element-parameters`; neither counts what it costs a chat, and neither covers the trailer. **For whoever fixes it:** the declared `provides` is a filter both doors already hold; whether the trailer comes once per batch rather than once per call is a wording decision as well as a size one, because it carries the undo rule (Article 8). **Not checked:** whether a later step reads an undeclared name - `StillTheHosts` serves both the answer and the chain, so the filter belongs on the reply until that is known; whether a proof record reads one; the share on a project-model job rather than a family build. | **FIXED 2026-10-11 by [#472](https://github.com/Ajmalpshaik/Heron-AI/pull/472), in the MCP server.** `_provides_lines` names a value that holds code - a delegate, a reflection or culture object, a namespace string, a list whose items print as System or vendor types - on one `NOT PRINTED` line instead of printing it. A value the card does not declare still prints when it holds data: 317 fragments' proofs show undeclared values that are real results, so printing only the declared ones was measured and rejected. Measured on the 177 replies of the 2026-10-09 chat: the results block 205,275 to 161,531 characters (-21%). The add-in's KEPT verdict paragraph is the add-in's to write and is unchanged. Held by `tests/test_read_door.py` section 7. NOT RUN in a live chat since the merge. **AND 2026-10-17, THE REST OF IT - a short change report, the detail on disk; compiled for all eight releases, NOT YET RUN IN REVIT.** The add-in now counts what Revit says a write changed - `DocumentChanged`, through `RevitChangedSet`, the collector the Keep-or-Discard box of D-126 already uses, extended rather than a second beside it, collected from `group.Start()` to just before the modeller is asked and Assimilate or the rollback, only `TransactionCommitted` on the target, a setup step's transactions (`label - stepName`) apart from the fragment's (`label`), each element's `Category.Name` read once (`HeronChangeReport`, proved in `tests/Heron.ChangeReport.TestHost`) - and the dispatcher keeps every element and the whole reply in `HeronPaths.Runs\<workflow id>.json`, DERIVED and pruned by `runs.retainDays`, with `elementsAdded`, `elementsModified`, `elementsDeleted` and `changeEvents` on the audit line (Article 22). `revit_change` prints two change lines and a `record:` line, the KEPT paragraph once per batch and the add-in's one sentence - the undo rule whole - on the rest, and counts rather than prints a value the last reply of the same capability showed word for word; `heron_run_record` (READ) pages a record back. **Replayed** - 172 kept changes in 39 batches shaped from `CREATE_FAMILY_EXTRUSION`'s code, through the real `revit_change` and an MCP SDK - 213,332 characters on `main` (ec400a37, re-measured after D-126) and 160,364 on this change, 24.8% less; an add-in from before the change prints byte for byte as it did. **For the owner, two wording decisions taken and his to reverse:** the KEPT paragraph is printed once per batch, a batch being calls less than 3 s apart (`heron_run_records.BATCH_GAP_S`), with the undo rule on every reply; and #472's "declared only" stays rejected - a value is left out only when the last reply showed it word for word and a run record holds it. The DRAFT line that used to follow every change is gone on its own account: since D-117 a capability that is not PROVEN is refused before anything is sent. Whether Revit raises the event where this needs it is [NEEDS-CHECKING Group EV](../needs-checking/group-ev.md). |
| **408** | **`.claude/skills/heron-session/bin/hook_log.py` LINE 39 HOLDS `\H` IN AN ORDINARY DOCSTRING - `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Heron\logs` - SO UNDER PYTHON 3.12 A HOOK THAT IMPORTS IT PRINTS *"SyntaxWarning: invalid escape sequence '\H'"* TO ITS STDERR.** The same class as [row 379](section-5b-rows-376-400.md), one file over. Seen 2026-10-09 in the session [row 5b-378](section-5b-rows-376-400.md) measured: two hook results in that chat's transcript carry it - the SessionStart hook's stderr, 204 bytes, and the PreToolUse hook's on the chat's only Write, 226 bytes. Reproduced 2026-10-10 on the owner's PC, Python 3.12.10, by compiling the file with warnings shown: `hook_log.py:39: SyntaxWarning: invalid escape sequence '\H'`. `heron_guard.py`, `main_moved.py` and `session_line.py` each import it to write their diary line. **It repeats because no Python 3.12 cache of the file is kept:** the main checkout's `bin/__pycache__` holds only `hook_log.cpython-311.pyc`, so every 3.12 run compiles it again - why 3.12 writes none there was not checked. **Cosmetic today:** the diary line is still written and no hook's decision changes. A later Python is due to make the sequence an error, and `import hook_log` sits inside guards written never to raise (`heron_guard.diary` catches `Exception`), so the hooks' diary would then stop without a word. A doubled backslash or a raw docstring ends it; row 379's test, `tests/test_placement.py` section 7, is the shape of the guard. **Recorded, not fixed:** this change records measurements only. | **FIXED 2026-10-11 by [#473](https://github.com/Ajmalpshaik/Heron-AI/pull/473).** The backslashes are doubled, and the same defect in `tests/test_bridge_roundtrip.py` and `tools/check-products.py`, found by compiling all 536 tracked Python files, is fixed with it. `tests/test_compiles_quietly.py` compiles every tracked file from source and fails on any warning: on main before the change it named all three files; after, 0 warnings. |
| **409** | **`EDIT_REVISION` TOOK ONE REVISION PER CALL, SO A JOB'S SEVEN REVISIONS COST A FIND AND AN EDIT EACH, EVERY PASS - AND THE ORDER THEY WENT IN DECIDED WHETHER HERON REFUSED.** Seen 2026-10-10 on the owner's job `4355-BHVD-3D-50C10-BL001A` (Revit 2020): seven revisions *IFI - Issued for Information*, Seq. 1 to 7. Issued by M.Sagheer, issued to A.Rahmani and the Issued tick went in one revision per call, Seq. 1 up to 7; un-issuing them and moving them from Numeric to Alphanumeric was REFUSED for Seq. 1 first - *"Moving Seq. 1 'IFI - Issued for Information' from numbering Numeric to Alphanumeric would renumber Seq. 2 … Seq. 7 …, which are ISSUED"* - and went through from Seq. 7 down; then all seven were issued again, one by one. Version 2 refuses a find that brings back two, by design, so every pass was seven finds, seven edits and seven entries in Revit's undo list. The owner asked why it went one by one. | **RUN 2026-10-17 on Revit 2020 - three `PASS`es on the scratch "Project1", every leg rolled back; SIGNED by Ajmal PS and PROVEN the same day (NEEDS-CHECKING Group BA, BA10 and BA11).** Several: `confirmCount=3` issued Seq. 1-3 in one call, `confirmCount=2` refused naming all three and both numbers. Issued: Seq. 4-6 un-issued and moved Numeric -> Alphanumeric in one call; a new issued by with no un-issue refused naming all three as ISSUED. One revision: Seq. 1 issued; asked for what it held, `alreadyThat` and nothing changed. Not yet seen: the one undo entry of a KEPT call (BA12). Before that: **BUILT, DRAFT 2026-10-10 - compiled 2020 to 2027, NOT yet run in Revit, so NOT proved.** `EDIT_REVISION` version 3 takes every revision the find hands in when the new value `confirmCount` equals the number found; several with it blank, or a count that disagrees, are refused with every one named and nothing changed. One call is one undo entry and all or nothing. The writes go un-issue (last sequence first), text columns, numbering (last first), show, issue (first sequence first) - the orders Revit took on the job - and the renumber check judges each revision by the state the set will be in when numbering is written, so the request refused for Seq. 1 goes through in one call. A lock or renumber refusal on any revision changes none of them. One revision answers as version 2 did, word for word, but `confirmCount` must be SENT, blank or not ([5b-227](section-5b-rows-226-250.md)); the two Group BA job files now send it. Routing measured before and after on scratch stores: the 26 held-out sentences answer identically on Revit 2020 and 2024. That measurement also showed, on main and unchanged here, *"who issued revision 3"* and *"what is the date of revision 2"* reaching EDIT_REVISION, *"show me the revision schedule"* CREATE_REVISION and *"what revisions are on this sheet"* SET_SHEET_REVISIONS - [5b-246](section-5b-rows-226-250.md)'s shape, recorded here, not fixed; EDIT_REVISION cannot change the model on a question, since every value empty is refused as *"nothing was asked for"*. An independent read of the code before push found a refusal for an unreadable `confirmCount` that named none of the revisions, and a read-back that throws after Revit took a value reported as refused (from version 2) - both fixed, the read-back now ends the call so the add-in rolls it back. Left as it was, from version 2: a numbering change counts a later ISSUED revision on *None* as renumbered though it prints no number, so it refuses where it need not - the safe direction, and BA5's question. **Owed:** [Group BA](../needs-checking/group-ba.md) BA10 to BA12 on a scratch project, never the job model, then Ajmal PS's signature; BA11's negative is also the issued-revision refusal [5b-412](section-5b-rows-401-425.md) says no run has measured. Version 2 was signed on Revit 2020 in #470 (fingerprint `b928479162dc855d`); this code moves under that proof, so EDIT_REVISION is DRAFT again and the proof block is kept to re-prove from. The Revit 2020 job file from #470 now sends `confirmCount` too. |
| **410** | **`ADD_FAMILY_CONNECTOR` FINDS NO FACE ON A FORM THAT A VOID HAS CUT, SO A FLANGED VALVE'S CONNECTOR COULD NOT GO ON ITS FLANGE.** Found 2026-10-10 building a fixed-size 4 inch OS&Y gate valve in Family1 (Revit 2020, session 36948, a new unsaved family). The two flange extrusions were cut by a bore void and two bolt-hole voids through `COMBINE_FAMILY_FORMS`, all in one combination. `ADD_FAMILY_CONNECTOR facePlane=Flange Face Left` then returned *"Nothing was made. No solid face lies on "Flange Face Left""*, although `REPORT_FAMILY_FORMS` read the flange at X -114.5 to -90.5 with the plane at X -114.5. Read in the source, not run: the fragment looks for the face in each form's own geometry, and once a form is in a combination Revit keeps its faces in the combination's geometry. **Worked round, not fixed:** a 1 mm ring (radius 51 to 80) on each flange face, in no combination; the connector then went on it - pipe, Global, 100 mm, at X -114.5, pointing -X. Every flanged or bored MEP family built this way meets it. **Fix when owned:** also search the faces of any combination the forms are in, keeping the exactly-one-face rule. | **FIXED IN CODE, DRAFT - not yet run in Revit (2026-10-11, PR #474).** [`ADD_FAMILY_CONNECTOR`](../../brain/fragments/add-family-connector/fragment.yaml) version 3 also reads every combination's geometry, as `COMBINE_FAMILY_FORMS` reads one, when no form's own face lies on the plane - exactly one, or refused with the count. A form's own face still wins, so the 1 mm ring keeps working, and the result says when joined faces lie on the same plane too. Whether Revit takes a connector on a joined face, and what that face's reference names, is reported as Revit answers - **unmeasured**; a refusal carries Revit's words and the ring as the way round. Compiled 2020 to 2027. Proof plan: [Group AN, AN12 to AN18](../needs-checking/group-an.md), on a scratch Metric Generic Model - negative a plane two faces of one combination lie on. |
| **411** | **`batch-prove` CANNOT JUDGE A FRAGMENT WHOSE RESULT IS A SENTENCE: `EDIT_REVISION` VERSION 2 CAME BACK `POSITIVE UNREADABLE` WITH BOTH LEGS HELD.** Found 2026-10-10 re-proving `edit-revision` on Project2 (a scratch project, Revit 2020, session 36948) with [the job file](../../tools/jobs/issue-revisions-revit-2020-2026-10-10.yaml). `changed` is declared a string result - version 2 made it one joined string so the reply prints all of it. The positive returned `changed` *issued by (blank) -> 'M.Sagheer'; issued to (blank) -> 'A.Rahmani'; issued*; the negative returned `changed` empty and `alreadyThat` naming the description and the date - a real contrast, read by hand. The runner still said *"POSITIVE UNREADABLE ... cannot be read as a quantity"*, because it judges a result by a count moving off zero. `heron_validate.py draft` drafted the proof all the same. **Fix when owned:** read a non-empty string result as found and an empty one as nothing, so every fragment that returns a sentence can be batch-proved. | **FIXED 2026-10-11.** A provide declared `type: string` **and** `role: result` is read as text: found when it says something, nothing when it is empty, blank or `(null)` - `string_results` and `_as_found` in [`brain/heron_validate.py`](../../brain/heron_validate.py), used by `positive_worked`, `looks_empty`, `draft`, `accept` and `batch-prove`'s `judge`. A string declared `role: accounting`, or with no role, is read as before, and `expect:` still decides what is judged. **Both legs, never one:** a one-word answer such as `SupplyAir` has the shape of a bare type name, which the negative skipped as a helper object, so reading it only in the positive would have PASSED the same word in both legs. **Beside a count or a flag, the count decides (D-51)** - found by review before merging: `set-material-colour` declares `changed: int` beside `wasColour` and `nowColour`, which read back the colour whether or not anything changed, so asked for the colour a material already has it answers `changed 0` with both filled, and the sentence alone would have PASSED it; the old signing gate's `_result_appeared` rescue signed exactly that shape. Now it is `POSITIVE EMPTY` in both, and `expect:` naming the sentence alone still judges it. [`tests/test_batch_prove.py`](../../tests/test_batch_prove.py) gained two sections and [`tests/test_validate_agent.py`](../../tests/test_validate_agent.py) one, for `accept` and `draft`; fourteen of their checks were seen to FAIL on the judge as it stood, none crashed, and breaking each call site in turn turns its own check red. **This row's own run record, re-judged:** `POSITIVE UNREADABLE` before, `PASS - positive: changed issued by (blank) -> 'M.Sagheer'; ...` after - and `batch-prove` now agrees with `accept`, which had let it through on `_result_appeared`'s comparison. **Every run record on the owner's laptop re-judged by both trees (310, measured 2026-10-11): five verdicts moved.** `edit-revision`, `create-mep-system-type` and `duplicate-view-template` went UNREADABLE to PASS (the last two already PROVEN through that same comparison). Two went stricter, and both are **seen, not fixed**: `report-family-parameters` went PASS to NEG NOT EMPTY - its negative returned `currentTypeName Standard`, the same word as its positive, which its contract declares `role: result`, and it passed before only because the word has no space in it; `report-findings` went UNREADABLE to NEG NOT EMPTY, its `report` writing *"NOTHING WAS CHECKED ..."* in the negative. Each is a contract calling context, or a sentence that always speaks, a result - owed a read of the contract (`role: accounting`, or proof by tracking), not changed here. **Also seen, not fixed:** in `draft_from_record`, a `provides()` that throws leaves `declared_names` unassigned, so the line after the `except` would raise `NameError`. **Not run in Revit:** judging needs none, and `edit-revision` is PROVEN, so `batch-prove` answers `ALREADY` for it now. |

N - description*, typed from an earlier read; when the owner deleted Seq. 1, Revit renumbered the rest and a name typed minutes before (Seq. 8) named nothing. A chain could not have done it: it cannot loop over dates, and every name it carries goes stale the same way. `EDIT_REVISION`'s half of the same job - issuing the seven one at a time - is row 5b-409 of [PR #469](https://github.com/Ajmalpshaik/Heron-AI/pull/469), not repeated here. | **FIXED at DRAFT 2026-10-11, NOT YET RUN IN REVIT.** New `CREATE_REVISIONS_FROM_SHEET_DATES` (FRG-SHT-029, MODIFY): one call, one Undo entry. It reads the sheets' own text notes with `LIST_DATES_ON_SHEETS`' parser, copied character for character (`tests/test_sheet_date_parser.py` holds the copies equal); reuses a revision already holding that date and description, else makes one, oldest date first; adds each to exactly its sheets, never replacing what a sheet lists; holds every revision by its id, so a sequence number is only read, for the report, after the last write; and rolls the whole call back if any revision vanished from the project. Refused with nothing changed when no sheet carries a date, the description is empty, or two revisions already stand for one date. Compiled for 2020 to 2027. The proof is [Group CZ](../needs-checking/group-cz.md), on Group CY's scratch projects, never on the owner's model. |

NEEDS REAL REVIT.** The test, on a scratch copy: wall 1 of a type whose core is material A, nothing painted; wall 2 of another type, with A painted on one face; ask for A over Walls with includePaint false, then true. The card is right if the answers are {1} then {1, 2}; the comments are right if they are {1} then {2}. If the comments are right, the repair is `FIND_UNUSED_MATERIALS`' - read both sets and union them when paint is asked for, at `:103` and `:237` - and the negative case should also expect the second answer to hold every element of the first. |

| **422** | **HERON COULD NOT READ, SET OR CHECK A FAMILY'S OMNICLASS NUMBER - `SET_FAMILY_SETTINGS` REFUSED IT AS "NOT ONE OF THE SETTINGS THIS SETS", NO LOOKUP FOUND ANYTHING, AND THE MODELLER SET IT BY HAND.** Found 2026-10-10 building a DN50 PN16 gate valve family (Pipe Accessories, Revit 2024) through Heron: `SET_FAMILY_SETTINGS` (FRG-DOC-036, PROVEN) refused "OmniClass Number", and `heron_lookup` on *"OmniClass Number"* and *"set the OmniClass number and title of this family"* named `RENUMBER_SEQUENTIAL` and `DRAW_FAMILY_DETAIL_LINES`, weak. On 2026-10-11, before this repair, *"set the OmniClass number and title of this family"* reached `CREATE_SHEETS`, *"which families have no OmniClass"* `DESCRIBE_ELEMENTS` and *"set the assembly code"* `CREATE_ASSEMBLY_VIEWS`. A grep for omniclass, `OMNICLASS_CODE` and `UNIFORMAT_CODE` found only `tools/api-surface/changes.json`. **Measured while repairing it, off the shipped assemblies:** `OMNICLASS_CODE`, `OMNICLASS_DESCRIPTION`, `UNIFORMAT_CODE` and `UNIFORMAT_DESCRIPTION` exist 2020 to 2025 and are gone at 2026, where the same numbers (-1002502, -1002503, -1002500, -1002501) are `CLASSIFICATION_CODE`, `CLASSIFICATION_DESCRIPTION`, `ASSEMBLY_CODE` and `ASSEMBLY_DESCRIPTION`, labelled "Classification Number" and "Classification Title" - so a fragment typing either spelling compiles on half the range; written into the [revit-version-support](../../.claude/skills/revit-version-support/SKILL.md) skill. No API reads the OmniClass list: it is `OmniClassTaxonomy.txt` to 2025 and `Classification_Taxonomy.txt` from 2026, TAB-separated code, title, level and built-in category id, in `Application.CurrentUsersDataFolderPath` with a cached copy in the release's `UserDataCache`, and the 2020-2025 file holds Windows-1252 bytes, not UTF-8. Only the per-user copy is read: the cache sits under a Windows special folder, which only `HeronPaths` may resolve (`tools/check-structure.py`), so a release whose per-user folder holds no copy answers "no file found" with the path it looked in. | **REPAIRED IN DRAFT, NOT YET RUN IN REVIT - by widening, no new capability (the owner's rule):** `SET_FAMILY_SETTINGS` v2 sets, changes and clears "OmniClass Number" against that file, only a code the file gives the family's own category, refusing any other with that category's codes listed; `REPORT_FAMILY_TEMPLATE` v2 reads the number and title and lists the codes for the category (`classificationCodes`); `CHECK_FAMILY_STANDARDS` v3 flags a blank code, one not in the file and one of another category, and checks Assembly Code and Keynote against Revit's own tables, in a family and in a project. Assembly Code and Keynote keep their setters - `SET_FAMILY_TYPE_VALUES` in a family, `SET_PARAMETER_VALUES_BY_ID` in a project - which take them as text; the check is what catches a wrong one. Setting the number on a family already loaded is not done inside any tool: the refusal names `OPEN_FAMILY_FOR_EDITING`, then `LOAD_FAMILY`, a reload the modeller agrees to by name (Article 7). The eight family methods now classify right after `SET_FAMILY_CATEGORY`. The two PROVEN cards went back to DRAFT. Proof owed: [NEEDS-CHECKING Group DB](../needs-checking/group-db.md). |

| **437** | **AN OMNICLASS NUMBER THAT `SET_FAMILY_SETTINGS` WROTE AND READ BACK WAS BLANK AGAIN FIVE MINUTES LATER, WITH NO HERON CALL IN BETWEEN - AND THE CARD'S "READ BACK, ALL OR NOTHING" COULD NOT HAVE SEEN IT, BECAUSE THE READ HAPPENS INSIDE ITS OWN OPEN TRANSACTION.** Seen 2026-10-12 on the owner's laptop, Revit 2020, in his family *TRG_Mechanical Equipment_Split Air Conditioner Outdoor Unit_Wall Mounted* (Mechanical Equipment, level-based, made from the IMPERIAL Generic Model template). **The order of events, from the chat and from this PC's Heron audit file** (`%APPDATA%\Heron\audit\audit-202610.jsonl`, one Revit session `2020/25812` throughout, times UTC - the laptop is UTC+3): 10:15:37 `REPORT_FAMILY_TEMPLATE` read; 10:15:48 `SET_FAMILY_SETTINGS` *"OmniClass Number=23.75.10.24.21.27.27"* answered *"OmniClass Number (blank) -> 23.75.10.24.21.27.27 Split System Air Conditioning Units - Revit filled OmniClass Title"*, KEPT; 10:18:34 `SET_FAMILY_FORM_SUBCATEGORY` put 33 forms on a new subcategory *Split Outdoor Unit* under Mechanical Equipment (`createdSubcategory` true), KEPT; 10:19:52 the owner pressed Ctrl+S (the file's saved time, 13:19:52 local); the owner then said the OmniClass had gone, and 10:21:21 `REPORT_FAMILY_TEMPLATE` read *OmniClass Number (blank)*; 10:21:30 `SET_FAMILY_SETTINGS` again; 10:21:39 the report read 23.75.10.24.21.27.27. **The audit holds no Heron call between 10:18:34 and 10:21:21**, and no read between 10:15:48 and 10:21:21 - so it is not known whether the number was already blank after the first call committed, after the subcategory call, or only after the save. What blanked it was Revit's own handling or something done by hand, not a later Heron call. Everything else in that family ran before the number was set: `SET_PROJECT_UNITS` (three calls in the audit, 09:34-09:36), `ADD_FAMILY_SHARED_PARAMETERS`, `SET_FAMILY_TYPE_VALUES`, `SET_FAMILY_FORMULA` x4, `DELETE_FAMILY_PARAMETERS` (unlabelFirst), `LABEL_FAMILY_DIMENSION` x4, `FLEX_FAMILY` (restored true). Whether the second write survived the owner's next save is not known. **Read in the code, not run in Revit.** The writer and the reader use one parameter on one element: [`set-family-settings/impl/any/fragment.cs`](../../brain/fragments/set-family-settings/impl/any/fragment.cs) finds `OMNICLASS_CODE` - `CLASSIFICATION_CODE` from 2026 - by name (lines 142-145), takes it from `doc.OwnerFamily` (lines 296, 367), writes it with `Parameter.Set` (line 487), regenerates, and reads the same element back (line 494); [`report-family-template/impl/any/fragment.cs`](../../brain/fragments/report-family-template/impl/any/fragment.cs) reads `doc.OwnerFamily.get_Parameter(clsCode)` (lines 212, 353 since its version 3). Neither goes through `FamilyManager`. [`set-family-form-subcategory/impl/any/fragment.cs`](../../brain/fragments/set-family-form-subcategory/impl/any/fragment.cs) calls `Categories.NewSubcategory` and sets `GenericForm.Subcategory` (lines 107, 122), and touches no parameter of the Family element - nothing in its code reaches the OmniClass Number. **The gap that IS in the code:** the read-back at line 494 runs inside the host's transaction, before it commits, so a value Revit takes back at commit, at a later regeneration or at save is still reported as read back and KEPT. **Four explanations, three still untested:** (a) Undo pressed twice by hand - that would take the subcategory away too, so whether the 33 forms are still on *Split Outdoor Unit* tells (a) from the rest. **Ruled out 2026-10-12 by the owner's word: the forms are still on *Split Outdoor Unit*.** Revit's undo list runs in order - the subcategory call would have to be undone before the number, and Redo brings them back in the same order - so no Undo and Redo leaves the forms moved and the number gone; (b) Revit 2020 also holds a family's OmniClass Number somewhere the write to the Family element does not reach - per type, say - and puts that back at save or regeneration (a guess about the API, unverified; if true, every OmniClass write on 2020 is unsafe, and [NEEDS-CHECKING DB11](../needs-checking/group-db.md) - the 2020 runs - has not run); (c) making a subcategory, or the regeneration after it, resets it (nothing in either fragment's code points that way); (d) Family Category and Parameters opened and closed with OK by hand. **First run of [DB14](../needs-checking/group-db.md), 2026-10-12, the owner's laptop, Revit 2020 (session 25812), a scratch family from the imperial Generic Model template saved as *Heron OmniClass Save Test* on his Desktop, NO family types:** `SET_FAMILY_CATEGORY` Mechanical Equipment, two `CREATE_FAMILY_EXTRUSION` boxes, then `REPORT_FAMILY_TEMPLATE` read as separate calls after each step - blank before; 23.75.10.24.21.27.27 *Split System Air Conditioning Units* after `SET_FAMILY_SETTINGS` committed; the same after `SET_FAMILY_FORM_SUBCATEGORY` made *Heron Test Part* and put both boxes on it; the same after the owner's Ctrl+S; the same after he closed the family and opened it again. **So in a family with no types neither the save, nor the subcategory, nor a close and reopen loses the number** - (c) does not reproduce, and (b) does not reproduce without types. New colours on a scheme other views show go on a copy unless the scheme is named. **New:** `REPORT_COLOR_FILL_SCHEME` (FRG-VIEW-136, READ) - each view's scheme per category, every row and colour, the legends, and the checks (no legend, two rows in one colour, values with no row, rows nothing uses, a template that holds the scheme); a new card because a read needs no transaction and no Changes switch. Measured 2026-10-13 by running the 63 suites that import the bridge or server code under a scratch audit hook that refused, and recorded, any open of the live Revit's pipe (`heron.2020.33484`): exactly those two reached for it - `test_mcp_serves.py` 1,842 attempts, `test_mcp_stdio.py` 121 through the server it starts - and both still PASS, so nothing ever said so. The call is `heron_resolve` -> `_revit_version` (`mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py`) -> `binding.sessions()` -> `bridge.discover()`, which pings every Revit in the real discovery folder. That timing is a match, not a recording: whether the sweep reached the pipe at 10:21:35 is not written anywhere. **That the suites reach a live Revit is its own defect, recorded and not fixed here.** **The defect, `revit/Heron.Bridge/BridgeServer.cs` `ListenLoopAsync`:** every accepted connection `Dispose`d the previous one whatever it was doing. One inside `Dispatch`, waiting on Revit's thread, carried on; its `WriteLineAsync` then threw on the disposed pipe and `ServeAsync`'s `catch (Exception)` swallowed it. docs/25 §3 recorded the gap on 2026-08-28 as *"the first chat loses one reply"* and left it for a machine that could compile the transport. **Reproduced without Revit**, with a scratch stand-in host compiled from the real `Heron.Bridge` and `Heron.Core` sources (one 0.5 s job at a time) and the real `heron_bridge_client`, called in the MCP server's own pattern - one lock, resolve and ping, the write, close: one chat alone, all 10 came back; the same chat while another process pinged every 150 ms, 30 calls - **not one came back**, 30 "lost", 16 of which had run; 20 connections at once - 1 came back, 19 lost, all 20 had run. **Two smaller holes of the same kind, found reading:** `RevitDispatcher.Dispatch` abandoned a job on the busy timeout while `Execute` checked `IsAbandoned` and then marked it started in a second step, so a job could be run AND reported *"Revit is busy and did not take the request"* - more likely behind a queue, because the busy clock starts at the enqueue; and `heron_config.response_timeout` was the operation timeout + 30 while the add-in answers within the busy wait + the operation wait, so a busy timeout above 30 (the settings allow up to the operation timeout) let the client give up first on a request that then ran. **Not checked:** the dispatcher race was never seen happen, only read; one displaced while waiting goes at once, as before; `PipeInstances` 2 to 16, and a full house waits for an instance rather than ending the listen loop. `RevitDispatcher`: `TryStart` and `TryAbandon` on one compare-and-swap - whichever side moves the job first wins, and a busy timeout that loses waits for the answer. `heron_config`: the client waits busy + operation + 20, still 90 at the defaults. **Measured on the stand-in with the fix:** the pinger case, all 30 came back; Moving the kind is a re-design of `SET_WALL_CONSTRAINTS`' card measured on every backend, or reopening row 109's "warn, don't re-rank" - the owner's call, as for rows 355 and 401. **Seen while measuring, the same on `main`, not this row's to fix:** *copy these walls to level 2 and level 3* reaches `SET_WALL_CONSTRAINTS` on lexical and `model` with and without #531; *select all the walls on level 2* reaches it on all three; *which level are these doors on* reaches `PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY` (`model`) and `CREATE_STAIRS` (re-ranker); *which level are these columns on* `PLACE_STRUCTURAL_FAMILY` (`model`); *what level is this duct on* and *which level is this duct on* `SET_MEP_INSULATION` (re-ranker); *how many ducts on each level* `CREATE_DUCT_RUNS` (re-ranker); *how many rooms on each level* `PLACE_ROOMS` (`model`, re-ranker); *what is the fire rating of these walls* `PLACE_WALL_SWEEPS` (`model`) and `SET_WALL_CONSTRAINTS` (re-ranker); *how thick are these walls* `SET_COMPOUND_LAYER_WIDTH` (lexical, re-ranker). **Still to show in Revit:** that `LookupParameter("Base Constraint")` finds the wall's parameter on an English Revit and that `AsValueString` gives the level's name - if it does not, the wall lands in `blank`, visibly, not as a wrong value. **Repeated on `main` `dcd1a3bc`** (556 fragments, after #530 and #529) against this branch merged into it, and with #531's head `adc03a76` on both sides, every backend, the same 90 sentences: the same answers and the same moves in every one. Answers that write go 49 to 43 on lexical, 60 to 54 on `model` and 58 to 54 with the re-ranker - `model` is one higher on both sides because between the two `main`s *list the wall types* moved from `SELECT_TYPES` to `SET_CURTAIN_WALL_MULLIONS`, which this change does not touch; the wall paraphrases go 17 to 15, 22 to 21 and 21 to 21; `SET_WALL_CONSTRAINTS` keeps all 12 of its own on every tree; and `test_routing_phrases.py` on the model fails the same 5 checks, with the four passing. **Gates, measured on `main` `2e6fe29c` against this branch, each tool on its own empty store and each backend apart:** `check-routing` exits 0 on all three, with the same claimed-and-not-reached lists (44 lexical, 54 `model`, 111 re-ranker) and, with the re-ranker, the same two declared questions answered by a write on both sides - not this change's; words #1 2625 of 3189 to 2627 of 3193; the only other moves are rank ripples of one to four in the contests over the filter's and the parameter reader's own sentences (*list the rooms*, the filter #12 to #8; *which walls are on this level* #3 to #2; *what size is this duct* #12 to #13), no answer changed. `check-risk-crossings` and `score-routing` give the same answer question for question on all three. `check-skill-routing` gives the same answers, with one nearness ripple on lexical and two runner-up ripples with the re-ranker. `check-intrusion` exits 0: on lexical `AUTO_SIZE_MEP` sits in one shortlist fewer (63 to 62) and the correlation stays 0.164; on `model`, with four more sentences in the corpus, `PLACE_WALL_SWEEPS` enters the top twelve at 57 and `CREATE_PARTS` and `PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY` go up one each, correlation 0.044 to 0.042; with the re-ranker `SET_WALL_CONSTRAINTS` enters the top twelve at 61 shortlists - the four sentences it answered are now another card's, and it still sits in their shortlists - `DIMENSION_ELEMENT_FACES` goes up one, correlation 0.059 to 0.061. `change-evidence` rules **NO CHANGE MEASURED** both in CI's lexical environment (296 suites, none failing, `test_routing_phrases.py` and `test_bridge_roundtrip.py` could not run, on both sides; for the routing fix nothing Revit runs changed. **`READ_ELEMENT_LEVEL` VERSION 2, DRAFT, NOT YET RUN IN REVIT** (FRG-ELE-011, asked for by the owner the same day): `WALL_BASE_OFFSET` joins the offset list, **last**, so it is asked only when none of the first three answered and nothing version 1 read an offset from can read a different one. Nothing else in `impl/` moved. The change makes version 1's proof stale, so the card is DRAFT and `check-signatures` lists it under STALE; its purpose text is unchanged, so the search reads the same words and only the PROVEN tie-break is lost - the 90 sentences answer exactly as before on all three backends with the card DRAFT. `WALL_BASE_OFFSET` is the parameter `SET_WALL_CONSTRAINTS` (PROVEN) already reads, and `tools/check-fragments-compile.py` compiles every fragment on all eight releases, 2020 to 2027, with .NET SDK 10.0.112. | **481** | **NO TOOL COULD RENAME A VIEW FILTER OR CHANGE ITS RULE, SO ON A LIVE PROJECT A RENAME AND A NEW RULE TOOK SIX UNDO STEPS AND LEFT A SECOND FILTER BEHIND.** Found 2026-10-15 on Revit 2020, session 25244, in a real project (never written to for proving): the owner asked for `OUTSEL_TRG_MECH_Level` (Levels; Type Name does not contain 'TRG_MEC_Level') to become `OUTSEL_TRG_MECH_Level_Grid` on Levels and Grids with Type Name does not contain 'TRG_MEC'. `SET_VIEW_FILTER_CATEGORIES` set categories only and kept the rule and the name; `CREATE_VIEW_FILTERS_BY_RULE` skips a name that exists; `RENAME_ELEMENTS` has no finder that hands it a filter. So it was done by replacement - `CREATE_VIEW_FILTERS_BY_RULE` version 2 made the new filter, `APPLY_VIEW_FILTER` put it on the two views, `REMOVE_VIEW_FILTER` took the old one off. | **WIDENED 2026-10-15 - `SET_VIEW_FILTER_CATEGORIES` version 2, DRAFT, NEEDS-CHECKING [Group EE](../needs-checking/group-ee.md).** An existing tool widened, no new capability or id: `newName`, `parameterName` and `matchValue` beside `categories`, each BLANK to keep what the filter has, any of the three in one transaction. The rule takes `CREATE_VIEW_FILTERS_BY_RULE` version 2's operator-led value ("does not contain TRG_MEC"); a blank `parameterName` keeps the one the rule tests. A new name or rule is all or nothing; a name another filter has is refused by name; a new rule with the categories kept must fit every one of them, where given categories keep version 1's leave-out-and-name. **`categories` became `IList<string>`**, read into categories by the fragment in the binder's order: the add-in's binder refuses a blank `IList<BuiltInCategory>` before a fragment runs, and teaching it otherwise is C# in `revit/` and a rebuild for eight releases. Callers type what they typed before. **THE RULE CODE IS A COPY, KEPT IN STEP BY HAND** - a fragment cannot call another's: the operator words, the factories by reflection, the parameter matched by label or built-in name and the read-back's words, the same as `CREATE_VIEW_FILTERS_BY_RULE` (whose `impl/` is untouched, so its proof holds) - and a third copy is in `CREATE_VIEW_FILTER` version 3 (PR #537, merged the same day, row 479). One shared rule builder would be a composition change, not this row's. The new values are required-but-blank, the house shape (`APPLY_VIEW_FILTER`'s `enabled`): both job files that run it pass `""`, read with `--dry-run` before and after (ALREADY, then WOULD RUN with the blanks); the dry run cannot see a missing value, so a caller that sends only the old two is refused before anything runs and told the three names. | **486** | **NO TOOL COULD DIMENSION TO AN ELEMENT INSIDE A LOADED LINK: EVERY WORD `CREATE_LINEAR_DIMENSION` VERSION 3 TOOK - GRID, LEVEL, PLANE, LINE, WALL, FAMILY, FACE - LOOKED ONLY IN THE HOST, AND THE OBVIOUS ROUTE, `Reference.CreateLinkReference`, HANDS BACK A REFERENCE REVIT WILL NOT DIMENSION TO.** Asked for on 2026-10-15 on a real project, Revit 2020, by the session setting up its builders-work plans: ten Generic Model cut-outs tagged in four host plans live in a LINKED model (one placed link; each publishes only CenterLeftRight, CenterFrontBack and weak references, so faces are the route, as in version 3), and the owner asked for a chain from grid C along the wall to both sides of each. Read only, never written. A grep of `brain/fragments` found no `CreateLinkReference` anywhere. **Widened, not added - version 4, the same capability and FRG id.** `between` takes one word more, in front of any other: `link LINK WORD ...`, LINK the placed link's id as Revit shows it, its unique id, or the linked model's name with or without `.rvt` (in quotes when it holds one of the words; the longest run of words before a word that names a link is the link). WORD is read exactly as version 3 reads it but inside the link's own document - its ids by the same `Id.ToString()` match, its names, its geometry read with references computed and no view (a host view cannot read a link's elements) at the host view's detail level - and every direction and point is carried into the host by the link's `GetTotalTransform`, so `face ID left` means left of the HOST's view. A link not loaded or hidden in the view is refused naming the links the host places; an id not in the link is refused, naming where it IS when it is anywhere - this model or another loaded link - so a host word on a linked id (`face 919517 bottom`) now says which link holds it - the one sentence version 3's id refusals gain, and only when the id is in a link (the refusal for a word it does not know also names `link`); a link inside a link and a link with no known word after it are refused by name. The read-back adds how many references Revit holds as linked elements (`Reference.LinkedElementId`). **MEASURED IN REVIT, NOT GUESSED - five probes on the owner's word, 2026-10-15**, on the READ path in his Revit 2020 with the real project open and handed no transaction: each made a box family, a saved link source and a host linking it, all with no window, drew every dimension in a transaction it rolled back, and closed all three. As `CreateLinkReference` hands it back, written `<link>:RVTLINK/<link type>:<element>...`, NewDimension refused every reference - "not geometric references" for a family's faces and planes and a grid, "invalid number of references" for a wall's faces - in a plan with a view template, one without, and a section. Rewritten `<link>:0:RVTLINK:<element>...` and parsed back, the same references were drawn and measured what was built: a linked box's face 2000 mm from a host grid, its centre plane 2500, a linked wall 200, a linked model line 1500. A whole element - a grid, a level, a reference plane - still failed until `:0:SURFACE` named its first surface: grid 500, level 1600, plane 1000, each as drawn (`:1:` measured -304.8 mm, nonsense). Faces read through `GetInstanceGeometry` measured right but read back as the family TYPE, so the symbol route version 3 already takes is kept. The fragment then ran 33 cases on those projects, the last after the final change: every positive as built - a mixed chain `500 + 500 + 500 + 500 + 1000`, the link rotated 90 degrees, by name and quoted - and every negative refused with nothing drawn; version 2's `grid C \| grid D` unchanged at 5000. **Compiled on all eight releases** with `tools/check-fragments-compile.py`; `CreateLinkReference`, `ParseFromStableRepresentation`, `ConvertToStableRepresentation`, `LinkedElementId`, `GetLinkDocument`, `GetTotalTransform`, `IsHidden(View)`, `GetCategoryHidden` and `Options.DetailLevel` read off the reference assemblies as the same 2020 to 2027 with `tools/api-surface --members`. Whether 2021 to 2027 hand back the same written form is NOT known: the rewrite touches only the two forms measured on 2020 and uses any other as it comes. **Routing**, 78 sentences frozen before anything was measured (the two asked for, 12 held-out ways of asking, 8 of versions 2 and 3's own job, 16 questions, 20 neighbouring requests, 20 from other domains), `main` against this branch on private stores (`c95b2fb0`, then again at `93e89aec` after `main` moved - the same answers): both asked-for sentences are declared and answer here by identity on spelling and on `model` (before: `DIMENSION_GRIDS_AND_LEVELS`, `AUDIT_MEP_OPENINGS`); 10 of the 12 held-out reach here on each backend - not *put a dimension string from grid C to the linked sleeves* (`DIMENSION_GRIDS_AND_LEVELS` on both), *add dimensions from the grid to the openings in the link* (`CREATE_GRIDS` on spelling, as on `main`), *dimension to the face of an element in the link* (`DIMENSION_ELEMENT_FACES` on `model`); on `model` version 3's *dimension the opening's width and height in this section* now comes here from `LABEL_FAMILY_DIMENSION`, where version 3 meant it to. No question moved to a write on any backend. Already on `main`, the same here and not this change's: *which dimension references a linked element* reaches this write on spelling and `model`, *what is the offset of the linked model* reaches `COPY_FROM_LINK`, and on `model` *what is the distance from grid C to the opening* reaches `CREATE_ANGULAR_DIMENSION`. One request moved between writes and is repaired: *dimension the openings in the electrical link from the grid*, declared, tipped the spelling backend's coin toss *add an angular dimension* (row 482 saw the same toss) from `CREATE_ANGULAR_DIMENSION` to `CREATE_DIMENSION` - measured with each sentence alone - so `CREATE_ANGULAR_DIMENSION` declares it now and answers it by identity on both backends (its proof covers its implementation only and stays valid). **With the re-ranker** - the owner's laptop, measured once 2.3 GB was free (cross-encoder confirmed on both runs) - the two declared sentences answer here by identity and NOTHING ELSE MOVED: all 76 other sentences answer as on `main`, so 10 of the 12 held-out stay where `main` sends them (`DIMENSION_GRIDS_AND_LEVELS`, `DIMENSION_ELEMENT_FACES` and others) - the re-ranker reads identity, capability and domain, and the identity is version 2's, left so on purpose; six questions reach writes there on `main` already (`MAXIMIZE_DATUM_EXTENTS`, `UNLOAD_LINKS`, `SPLIT_MEP_RUN`, `SET_REFERENCE_PLANE_LENGTH`, this card for *which dimensions are in this view*), unchanged. `drawing-set` step 8 names the linked form; `DIMENSION_GRIDS_AND_LEVELS`'s routing table names the route. | **WIDENED, NOT ADDED - version 4 of FRG-VIEW-101, DRAFT.** Run in Revit 2020 on scratch projects, not proved: [NEEDS-CHECKING Group EH](../needs-checking/group-eh.md) is the proof, on new projects by hand, with `tools/jobs/create-linear-dimension-link-2026-10-15.yaml` (dry run: five jobs runnable). | **523** | **A FRAGMENT THAT LOOPED FOR EVER HELD REVIT UNTIL REVIT WAS ENDED: THE DISPATCHER COULD ONLY ANSWER `still_running`, AND THE STOP THAT MIGHT HAVE HELPED WAS READ ONLY BEFORE A JOB STARTED AND SET BY NOTHING.** A fragment runs on Revit's own thread inside `Execute`, and Revit offers no way into that thread from outside. `RevitDispatcher` stopped waiting after `revit.operationTimeoutSeconds` and answered *"It is still working; do not repeat the request"* - true, and of no help: the loop went on, Revit stayed frozen, and the modeller's only way out was ending Revit with the model unsaved. `HeronStop` said of itself *"Nothing can set this flag today"*, and [D-46](../decisions/D-46.md) recorded both of its gates as inert. And a fragment that catches `Exception` - much of the library does - would have swallowed any throw put in its way. | **FIXED 2026-10-17 in the add-in, [D-129](../decisions/D-129.md) - compiled for all eight releases, NOT YET RUN IN REVIT.** `HeronStackGuard` puts `HeronRunClock.Check()` at the head of every loop body and at the entry of every method and lambda; the clock trips on the dispatcher's own wait less a margin (50 s of 60) or on a stop asked during the job, and stays tripped, and `RevitFragment` asks it after every script, thrown or not, rolls the job back and answers `ran_too_long` or `stopped_mid_run`, saying whether Revit confirmed the rollback. A new `stop` operation, declared Read, is answered by the dispatcher before the queue so it reaches a Revit a runaway holds; nothing over the bridge resumes, and a restart of Revit clears it, which `HeronStop.Message` now names. `tests/test_stack_guard.py`: every loop in the library asks the clock, the rewrite adds nothing but its two calls and the braces a one-statement body needs, and guarded `while(true){}` and `try{while(true){}}catch(Exception){}` stop at about 200 ms on a 200 ms deadline. **LEFT OPEN:** `tools/check-fragments-compile.py` compiles each fragment WITHOUT the rewrite, so a guarded fragment is first compiled against a release's Revit assemblies inside Revit - the gap D-28 warns of, narrowed by the test host's token-for-token check and not closed; an expression-bodied lambda, a LINQ query and a `goto` loop carry no check; a single long Revit API call cannot be interrupted; and nothing in the MCP server sends `stop` yet. Whether a stop should survive a restart was [Q-68](../open-questions/from-master-specification-part-2.md); the owner answered the same day, [D-130](../decisions/D-130.md): a restart clears it. NEEDS-CHECKING [Group EZ](../needs-checking/group-ez.md). |
| **540** | **NO HERON TOOL COULD PUT ONE VIEW WHERE ANOTHER IS, ON EVERY SHEET IT IS ON - THE AS-BUILT LEGEND SWAP.** Found 2026-10-17 in the chat *"Shop drawings note verification"*, on a real project in Revit 2020: the shop-drawing legend NOTES sits on four sheets, a copy AS-BUILT NOTES carries the as-built wording, and at the as-built issue it has to go exactly where NOTES is on every sheet. `PLACE_VIEW_ON_SHEET` placed a view at a typed point on one sheet, `PLACE_VIEWS_ON_SHEET` laid views out on a grid, `PLACE_SCHEDULE_ON_SHEET` placed a schedule at a point - none takes a view off, none reads where another view sits, so the swap was done by hand sheet by sheet. Measured on `main` `5fce1f2`: none of *"swap the NOTES legend for AS-BUILT NOTES on all sheets"*, *"replace this view on the sheets with that one"* or *"put this legend where the old one is"* reached a sheet-placing tool on any of four backends - they went to `LIST_DATES_ON_SHEETS`, `SET_SHEET_TITLE_BLOCK`, `LIST_SHEET_SETS`, `PLACE_VIEWS_ON_SHEET` and `CREATE_LEGEND_VIEW`, which makes a NEW legend. **Why `PLACE_VIEW_ON_SHEET` and not the plural:** its `views` is typed by name; the plural's views arrive only from a chain or the selection, and its whole point is a grid. **API, read with `tools/api-surface` one release at a time:** `Viewport.LabelOffset` and `ViewSchedule.IsSplit` exist from 2022; `Viewport.ViewId` has a SETTER from 2023 - not used, because 2020 to 2022 cannot and one path keeps every release the same run. | **WIDENED 2026-10-17 - DRAFT, NOT RUN IN REVIT (NEEDS-CHECKING [Group FC](../needs-checking/group-fc.md)).** `PLACE_VIEW_ON_SHEET` version 2 (was PROVEN; version 1's proof kept on the card as a record, stale): a new optional `oldViewId` - `optional: true`, `absent: none`, so no new required input - and `sheetId` may now be left out the same way, meaning every sheet the old view is on. A viewport is replaced, not changed: a new one at the old box centre, the old viewport type, rotation and (2022 and later) title position, then the old one deleted and its detail number and pin carried over; a schedule takes the old one's corner. `at` is an offset in mm, `0,0,0` the job. Each sheet is a SubTransaction - all or nothing, the old view kept where a sheet fails - and read back: new `swapped` lines (sheet number, old gone, new there, centre or corner difference in mm, viewport type) and new `notDone` reasons; `refused` holds the sheets left unchanged. More than 0.1 mm off is not kept. A split schedule (2022 and later) and a title block's revision schedule are refused. The add-in resolves `oldViewId` by view name (`RevitFragment.OneIdNamed`, one name added). Compiled: the fragment on all eight releases 2020 to 2027 (`tools/check-fragments-compile.py`) and the add-in on all eight (`tools/check-compile.py`). Proof plan `tools/jobs/place-view-on-sheet-swap-2026-10-17.yaml`, dry-run clean: version 1's placing job, a schedule on one sheet, and THE PROOF - a legend on two sheets with different viewport types; the negative is a drafting view already on another sheet, which must change nothing. **Routing, measured on four backends against `main` `cfde9bd8`** (this branch merged with it) - lexical (CI's), the trained model `potion-base-8M`, and each with the cross-encoder re-ranker - each sentence through `heron_retrieve.find`, fresh scratch stores: the three sentences above reach this card by identity on all four; of eleven ways of asking the swap, 5 reach it on lexical, 6 with the re-ranker, 5 on the model, 6 on the model with the re-ranker (0 or 1 on main). **No question moved onto this card against main, and no change request moved onto a read**; `tools/check-risk-crossings.py` answers the same on both. *"is this view already on a sheet"* reached this card with the re-ranker on main as well; `LIST_VIEWS_ON_SHEET` version 2 (row 5b-560, merged the same day) answers it with `viewName`, so it is declared there and reaches that read on all four. Three measured choices: the version 2 line in `purpose:` says *replace*, not *put*, because *put* lifted this card past `SET_SELECTION` for *"put them on the screen"* on the trained model and failed `tests/test_embed.py` (0.381 against 0.379); the job's long explanation is a comment, not `purpose:`, because in `purpose:` it pulled *"show me the sheets with the notes legend"* and *"how many sheets have the notes legend"* off a read onto this change on the model backend and took *"add the view to the drawing sheet"* from #2 to #15 by keywords; and two more sentences (*"swap the legend on all the sheets"*, *"replace the legend on every sheet with the as-built one"*) were tried and left out, because they pulled *"is the AS-BUILT NOTES legend on any sheet"* and *"where is the NOTES legend placed"* onto it. **Still open, recorded not fixed:** (a) *"has this view been placed on a sheet"* reaches this card with the re-ranker on main and branch alike - not declared on `LIST_VIEWS_ON_SHEET`, whose card records that *placed* in its indexed text pulled change requests onto it; *"is this legend already placed"* reaches `CREATE_LEGEND_VIEW` everywhere, as on main; (b) some other ways of asking the swap still reach `CREATE_LEGEND_VIEW`, `SET_SHEET_TITLE_BLOCK` or `CREATE_REVISION`, as on main; (c) `check-routing` lets this card outrank `PLACE_VIEWS_ON_SHEET`' own *"put these views on a sheet"* by keywords - it is answered by identity, so `find` still sends it home on all four backends. No new capability and no new id. Before the write each face is measured off the element's geometry (its outermost point that way; hidden geometry only when it has no visible geometry); after the write and a regeneration it is measured again, the element is moved back by exactly the difference with `ElementTransformUtils.MoveElement` in the SAME transaction - **one undo entry** - and measured a third time; a face not back within 0.1 mm throws and every row is taken back. Nothing about the move reads the value, so the 2.25 mm trap cannot reach it. `outside` is found from the wall the element sits in (this model or a loaded link) and the three signs the owner measured on all 18 openings: no STRUCTURAL floor 1 m and 3 m out on that side at the element's level (this model and loaded links) with one on the other; a thin linked wall (at most half the host wall's thickness) on that side only; this model's ducts and duct accessories within 3 m on the other side. A sign with nothing to go on does not vote; none voting, or two disagreeing, refuses the element naming what each found. Refused before anything is written: pinned, in a model group, no geometry, a word that is no face, a missing or sheet-like view. By id rows a refusal stops the run (all or nothing, as version 2); by key it refuses that row. readBack gains each element's `face` (before, how far the write moved it, the move, now, off by, and the signs); resultJson gains `keepFace`. **Left out it is version 2, statement for statement:** every new line is behind `faceKeeper`, which is null, so resultJson, written, stale, refused and findings are version 2's; the reply's list of what the fragment left gains one line, `faceKeeper (null)`. Written into the card. **Routing, main against branch, four backends:** both sentences are declared on the card and now reach it by identity on lexical, model2vec, lexical + cross-encoder and model2vec + cross-encoder; of every sentence any card declared that day plus the seven below - 3,343 measured - nothing else moved on lexical or model2vec, and of the 119 that have this card anywhere in their top 20, nothing else moved with the cross-encoder; the owner's 79 questions (`tests/data/owner-questions.yaml`) - none moved on any of the four.
